<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that, for every smooth integral complex projective surface <i>S</i> and every ample line bundle <i>L</i>, the multipoint Seshadri constant at <i>r</i> very general points equals $`\sqrt{L^2/r}`$ for every sufficiently large integer <i>r</i>. This resolves positively the qualitative Nagata–Biran conjecture for surfaces.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every smooth integral complex projective surface $S$ and every ample line bundle $L$ on $S$, there is an integer $r_0=r_0(S,L)$ with the following property. For each integer $r\ge r_0$, there is a countable union $Z_r$ of proper Zariski-closed subsets of $U_r$, with nonempty complement, such that for every $\mathbf p\in U_r\setminus Z_r$ the class $$\pi^*L-\sqrt{\frac{L^2}{r}}\sum_{i=1}^r E_i$$ is nef on the blow-up at $\mathbf p$. Consequently, $$\varepsilon(S,L;\mathbf p)=\sqrt{\frac{L^2}{r}}.$$
  - Explanation: Eventual maximality holds at very general tuples on each polarized surface; the threshold depends on the surface and polarization.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rank-persistence)): Let $m,r,N$ be positive integers and let $q_1,\ldots,q_r$ be distinct points of an open subset $U\subset\mathbb C^2$. For $1\le i\le N$ and $0\le s<s_0$, let $f_{i,s}$ be holomorphic on $U$. Suppose that, as $s\downarrow0$, every derivative of $f_{i,s}$ of total order less than $m$ at each $q_j$ converges to the corresponding derivative of $f_{i,0}$. If the jet matrix of $f_{1,0},\ldots,f_{N,0}$ has column rank $N$, then the jet matrix of $f_{1,s},\ldots,f_{N,s}$ also has column rank $N$ for all sufficiently small positive $s$.* *In particular, convergence locally uniformly on $U$ suffices for the derivative hypothesis.
  - Explanation: A nonzero maximal minor of the limiting finite jet matrix stays nonzero under sufficiently small perturbations.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-initial-transfer)): Let $F\subset\mathbb C\{x,z\}$ be a finite-dimensional complex vector space of convergent holomorphic germs, and let $t>0$. The set $E_t(F)$ has exactly $\dim F$ elements, and $F$ admits a basis whose initial exponents are these distinct elements.* *Let $r,m$ be positive integers. If the monomial space $$\mathop{\mathrm{span}}_{\mathbb C}\{X^\alpha Z^\beta:(\alpha,\beta)\in E_t(F)\}$$ has an injective order-$m$ jet test at some distinct points $q_1,\ldots,q_r\in(\mathbb C^\times)^2$, then representatives of $F$ on a common neighborhood of the origin have an injective order-$m$ jet test at distinct points arbitrarily close to the origin.
  - Explanation: Coefficient elimination selects distinct initial monomials, and weighted dilation transfers their injective jet test to the original germs.
- **C4** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-node)): Let $S$ be a smooth integral complex projective surface and let $L$ be an ample line bundle. There exist a positive integer $d$, an integral divisor $V\in\|dL\|$, and a point $p\in V$ such that $V$ is smooth away from $p$ and has two smooth transverse analytic branches at $p$.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently positive linear system on the point blow-up supplies an integral divisor with a transverse node after blow-down.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-quadrilateral)): For the surface, line bundle, nodal divisor, coordinates and frame fixed above, every integer $k\ge1$, every $t>T$, and every nonzero $s\in H^0(S,kL)$ satisfy $$\nu_t(s)\in kQ_t.$$ Moreover, $H^0(S,kL)$ has a basis with pairwise distinct initial exponents in $kQ_t$; the basis is empty if this section space is zero.
  - Explanation: Branch intersections bound the initial exponent of a residual section; removing nodal-divisor factors gives the full quadrilateral bound.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-slices)): For $-b\le q\le a$, the slice of $Q_t$ on the line $\alpha-\beta=q$ has length in the $\beta$ coordinate equal to $$\ell_t(q)= \begin{cases} c(1+q/b),&-b\le q\le0,\\[2pt] c(1-q/a),&0\le q\le a. \end{cases}$$ There are no slices outside this interval, and $$P_t=\{(q,h):-b\le q\le a,\ 0\le h\le\ell_t(q)\}.$$
  - Explanation: The diagonal slice lengths form the tent defining the compressed triangle.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-compression)): Let $k,m,r$ be positive integers. If $\mathcal M(kP_t)$ has injective order-$m$ jets at some $r$ distinct points of $(\mathbb C^\times)^2$, then $\mathcal M(kQ_t)$ has injective order-$m$ jets at some $r$ distinct points of $(\mathbb C^\times)^2$.
  - Explanation: A triangular slice basis degenerates to distinct monomials in the triangle, transferring full column rank to the quadrilateral.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-surface-transfer)): Let $k,m,r$ be positive integers. If $\mathcal M(kP_t)$ has injective order-$m$ jets at some $r$ distinct torus points, then $H^0(S,kL)$ has injective order-$m$ jets at some $r$ distinct points of $S$.
  - Explanation: Diagonal compression followed by initial-monomial transfer carries the triangle test to global sections.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-triangle-multiplicity)): Let $\rho>0$, and let $\Delta\subset\mathbb R^2$ be a triangle of area $\rho^2/2$ and vertical range $\rho$. Every nonzero Laurent polynomial $F\in\mathbb C[U^{\pm1},Z^{\pm1}]$ whose exponent support is contained in $\Delta$ satisfies $$\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{(1,1)}F\leq\rho.$$
  - Explanation: Factoring the common horizontal vanishing order leaves a restriction whose vertical exponent span bounds the remaining order.
- **C10** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-subgroup-test)): Let $H>0$, let $r$ be a positive integer, and let $P\subset\mathbb R^2$ be a triangle of area $H/2$. Suppose there is a primitive vector $u\in\mathbb Z^2$ such that $$\max_{\xi\in P}\det(u,\xi) -\min_{\xi\in P}\det(u,\xi)=\sqrt{rH}.$$ Then there is a subgroup $G\subset(\mathbb C^\times)^2$ of exactly $r$ distinct points such that, for all positive integers $k,m$ with $m>k\sqrt{H/r}$, the order-$m$ jet test on $\mathcal M(kP)$ at $G$ is injective.
  - Explanation: Character projections separate lattice cosets, and the triangle multiplicity estimate excludes a nonzero jet kernel above the critical slope.
- **C11** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-direction)): Fix $d,H>0$ and $T>1$, and define $P_t$ as above for $t>T$. For every sufficiently large integer $r$ there are $t>T$ and a primitive vector $u\in\mathbb Z^2$ such that the range of $\det(u,\cdot)$ on $P_t$ has length $\sqrt{rH}$. The threshold depends only on $d,H,T$.
  - Explanation: A power-of-two coordinate and an odd coordinate yield a primitive direction while continuous shape variation achieves the required determinant range.
- **C12** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-torus-test)): For the fixed parameters $d,H,T$ of Section <a href="#sec:node" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:node">3</a>, there is an integer $r_0$ such that for every integer $r\geq r_0$ one can choose a single $t>T$ and a subgroup $G\subset(\mathbb C^\times)^2$ of $r$ distinct points for which the following holds: for all positive integers $k,m$ satisfying $$m>k\sqrt{H/r},$$ the order-$m$ jet test on $\mathcal M(kP_t)$ at $G$ is injective.
  - Explanation: The torus subgroup and shape are fixed before the section degree and jet order are chosen.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-jet-open)): Let $S$ be a smooth integral complex projective surface, let $L$ be a line bundle, and let $r,k,m$ be positive integers. In the configuration space $$U_r=\{(p_1,\ldots,p_r)\in S^r:p_i\ne p_j\text{ for }i\ne j\},$$ the tuples at which the order-$m$ jet test on $H^0(S,L^{\otimes k})$ is injective form a Zariski-open subset. If each test in a countable collection with fixed $S,L,r$ is injective at some tuple, then all those tests are simultaneously injective at very general tuples. Their common locus is nonempty.
  - Explanation: Maximal minors define open injective loci, and Baire category yields simultaneous tests at very general tuples.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-positive-square)): Let $Y$ be a smooth integral complex projective surface, let $A$ be a nef real divisor class, and let $D$ be a rational Cartier divisor. If $D^2>0$ and $A\cdot D>0$, then $H^0(Y,\mathcal O_Y(kD))\ne0$ for every sufficiently large positive integer $k$ divisible by the denominators of $D$.
  - Explanation: Serre duality removes the top cohomology and positive-square Riemann–Roch growth supplies effective divisible multiples.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-homogeneous-nef)): Let $S$ be a smooth integral complex projective surface, let $L$ be ample, and let $p_1,\ldots,p_r$ be distinct points. Put $H=L^2$ and $w=\sqrt{H/r}$. Suppose that, for all positive integers $k,m$ with $m>kw$, no nonzero section of $L^{\otimes k}$ vanishes to order at least $m$ at every $p_i$. On the blow-up $\pi:Y\to S$ of these points, with exceptional curves $E_i$, the real divisor class $$D_0=\pi^*L-w\sum_{i=1}^rE_i$$ is nef.
  - Explanation: A hypothetical negative curve permits a rational positive-square perturbation, producing a section that contradicts the homogeneous jet tests.
- **C16** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sesha-upper)): For every ample line bundle $L$ on a smooth integral complex projective surface $S$ and every tuple of $r$ distinct points, $$\varepsilon(S,L;p_1,\ldots,p_r)\le\sqrt{L^2/r}.$$
  - Explanation: Asymptotic section and jet dimensions produce effective divisors that bound every nef exceptional coefficient.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-fields)): Let $K$ be an uncountable algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, let $S$ be a smooth integral projective surface over $K$, and let $L$ be an ample line bundle on $S$. There is an integer $r_0=r_0(S,L)$ such that, for every $r\ge r_0$, outside a countable union of proper Zariski-closed subsets of $U_r$, with nonempty complement, one has $$\varepsilon(S,L;p_1,\ldots,p_r)=\sqrt{L^2/r}.$$
  - Explanation: Finite-data descent and flat base change carry the injective loci to an uncountable algebraically closed field of characteristic zero.

          "source_id": "PM-039.2"
        }

        "exact_quote": "Let $H>0$, let $r$ be a positive integer, and let $P\\subset\\mathbb R^2$ be a triangle of area $H/2$.         "source_id": "PM-039.2"
      },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **multipoint Seshadri constant** ([source](#eq:upper-bound)): For a tuple of distinct points $\mathbf p=(p_1,\ldots,p_r)$, the multipoint Seshadri constant is $$\varepsilon(S,L;\mathbf p) =\inf_C\frac{L\cdot C}{\sum_{i=1}^r\mathop{\mathrm{mult}}_{p_i}C},$$ where $C$ ranges over integral curves meeting at least one of the points.
- **nef real divisor class** ([source](#eq:upper-bound)): Here a real divisor class is *nef* if it has nonnegative intersection with every integral curve.
- **very general tuples** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A property holds at *very general* tuples if its failures are contained in a countable union of proper Zariski-closed subsets of $U_r$.
- **order of vanishing** ([source](#sec:jets)): For a holomorphic function $f$ near a point $q$ of a smooth complex surface, write $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_q(f)$ for its order of vanishing, with $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_q(0)=\infty$.
- **order-m jet map** ([source](#sec:jets)): For distinct points $\boldsymbol q=(q_1,\ldots,q_r)$ and a finite-dimensional space $F$ of functions defined near them, its *order-$m$ jet map* is $$J^m_{\boldsymbol q}\colon F\longrightarrow \bigoplus_{j=1}^r\mathcal O_{q_j}/\mathfrak m_{q_j}^{m}, \qquad f\longmapsto (f\bmod\mathfrak m_{q_j}^{m})_{j=1}^r.$$
- **injective jet test** ([source](#sec:jets)): We call the corresponding jet test *injective* when this map is injective, or equivalently when no nonzero $f\in F$ vanishes to order at least $m$ at every $q_j$.
- **initial exponent** ([source](#sec:jets)): Thus each nonzero series $f\in\mathbb C\{x,z\}$ has a first nonzero exponent in this order, denoted by $\nu_t(f)$.
- **initial exponent set** ([source](#sec:jets)): For a finite-dimensional subspace $F\subset\mathbb C\{x,z\}$, put $$E_t(F)=\{\nu_t(f):0\ne f\in F\}.$$
- **compressed triangle** ([source](#sec:compression)): We next reduce its jet tests to those of the triangle $$P_t=\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}\{(-b,0),(a,0),(0,c)\}.$$
- **vertical range** ([source](#pm-h-9)): For a bounded subset of $\mathbb R^2$, its *vertical range* is the length of its projection onto the second coordinate axis.
- **primitive lattice vector** ([source](#pm-h-10)): A vector in $\mathbb Z^2$ is *primitive* if its coordinates are coprime.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | multipoint Seshadri constant | ≤ | $\sqrt{\frac Hr}$ | Every distinct tuple on the polarized surface. |

| Q3 | quadratic weight expression | > | $d^2Ht$ | Weights above the fixed threshold. |
| Q4 | quadrilateral coordinate c | = | $\frac1d$ | Fixed positive nodal-divisor degree. |
| Q5 | scaled intersection parameter W | = | $dH$ | Fixed nodal-divisor degree and polarization self-intersection. |
| Q6 | quadrilateral intercept a | = | $\frac{Wt}{1+t}$ | Permitted weights for the nodal diagram. |
| Q7 | quadrilateral intercept b | = | $\frac{W}{1+t}$ | Permitted weights for the nodal diagram. |
| Q8 | compressed triangle area | = | $H/2$ | Every permitted weight for the fixed nodal diagram. |
| Q9 | Laurent polynomial vanishing order | ≤ | $\rho$ | Nonzero support inside a triangle with the specified area and vertical range. |
| Q10 | determinant projection range | = | $\sqrt{rH}$ | A primitive direction satisfying the exact projection hypothesis. |
| Q11 | jet order | > | $k\sqrt{H/r}$ | Positive section degrees and jet orders, with the subgroup fixed for the point count. |
| Q12 | jet bundle rank | = | $\binom{m+1}{2}$ | Jets of orders less than the chosen positive order on a surface. |
| Q13 | boundary class self-intersection | = | $0$ | The boundary exceptional coefficient is the critical square-root slope. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sesha-upper-2): Eventual maximality holds at very general tuples on each polarized surface; the threshold depends on the surface and polarization. -> Eventual maximality holds at very general tuples on each polarized surface; the threshold depends on the surface and polarization.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-rank-persistence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rank-persistence): A nonzero maximal minor of the limiting finite jet matrix stays nonzero under sufficiently small perturbations. -> A nonzero maximal minor of the limiting finite jet matrix stays nonzero under sufficiently small perturbations.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-initial-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-initial-transfer): Coefficient elimination selects distinct initial monomials, and weighted dilation transfers their injective jet test to the original germs. -> Coefficient elimination selects distinct initial monomials, and weighted dilation transfers their injective jet test to the original germs.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-node) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-node): A sufficiently positive linear system on the point blow-up supplies an integral divisor with a transverse node after blow-down. -> A sufficiently positive linear system on the point blow-up supplies an integral divisor with a transverse node after blow-down.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-prop-quadrilateral) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-quadrilateral): Branch intersections bound the initial exponent of a residual section; removing nodal-divisor factors gives the full quadrilateral bound. -> Branch intersections bound the initial exponent of a residual section; removing nodal-divisor factors gives the full quadrilateral bound.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-slices) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slices): The diagonal slice lengths form the tent defining the compressed triangle. -> The diagonal slice lengths form the tent defining the compressed triangle.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-prop-compression) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-compression): A triangular slice basis degenerates to distinct monomials in the triangle, transferring full column rank to the quadrilateral. -> A triangular slice basis degenerates to distinct monomials in the triangle, transferring full column rank to the quadrilateral.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-cor-surface-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-surface-transfer): Diagonal compression followed by initial-monomial transfer carries the triangle test to global sections. -> Diagonal compression followed by initial-monomial transfer carries the triangle test to global sections.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-lem-triangle-multiplicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-triangle-multiplicity): Factoring the common horizontal vanishing order leaves a restriction whose vertical exponent span bounds the remaining order. -> Factoring the common horizontal vanishing order leaves a restriction whose vertical exponent span bounds the remaining order.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-prop-subgroup-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-subgroup-test): Character projections separate lattice cosets, and the triangle multiplicity estimate excludes a nonzero jet kernel above the critical slope. -> Character projections separate lattice cosets, and the triangle multiplicity estimate excludes a nonzero jet kernel above the critical slope.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-lem-direction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-direction): A power-of-two coordinate and an odd coordinate yield a primitive direction while continuous shape variation achieves the required determinant range. -> A power-of-two coordinate and an odd coordinate yield a primitive direction while continuous shape variation achieves the required determinant range.
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-cor-torus-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-torus-test): The torus subgroup and shape are fixed before the section degree and jet order are chosen. -> The torus subgroup and shape are fixed before the section degree and jet order are chosen.
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-lem-jet-open) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-jet-open): Maximal minors define open injective loci, and Baire category yields simultaneous tests at very general tuples. -> Maximal minors define open injective loci, and Baire category yields simultaneous tests at very general tuples.
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-lem-positive-square) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-positive-square): Serre duality removes the top cohomology and positive-square Riemann–Roch growth supplies effective divisible multiples. -> Serre duality removes the top cohomology and positive-square Riemann–Roch growth supplies effective divisible multiples.
- **R15** [claim](#stmt-prop-homogeneous-nef) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-homogeneous-nef): A hypothetical negative curve permits a rational positive-square perturbation, producing a section that contradicts the homogeneous jet tests. -> A hypothetical negative curve permits a rational positive-square perturbation, producing a section that contradicts the homogeneous jet tests.
- **R16** [claim](#stmt-lem-sesha-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sesha-upper): Asymptotic section and jet dimensions produce effective divisors that bound every nef exceptional coefficient. -> Asymptotic section and jet dimensions produce effective divisors that bound every nef exceptional coefficient.
- **R17** [claim](#stmt-cor-fields) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-fields): Finite-data descent and flat base change carry the injective loci to an uncountable algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. -> Finite-data descent and flat base change carry the injective loci to an uncountable algebraically closed field of characteristic zero.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.roadmap.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **slice-compression**: Each diagonal integer slice has consecutive exponents; moving its lower endpoint to height zero places the resulting exponents in the compressed triangle.
- **triangle-area**: The compressed triangle has area $H/2$.
- **eventual-nefness**: The boundary class is nef at very general tuples once the point count exceeds the polarized-surface threshold.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **finite-versus-universal**: Treat successful finite diagram checks as a certificate of nefness on an arbitrary blow-up. -> The universal section-vanishing hypothesis has not been established by enumerating a finite diagram.
- **particular-tuples**: Return the maximal Seshadri value for a supplied tuple merely because its points are distinct. -> The supplied tuple may belong to the exceptional locus.
- **compress-all-coefficients**: Replace all Taylor coefficients of a section by its bounded initial diagram. -> The exponent bound controls initial terms and does not bound the complete Taylor support.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The diagonal-slice construction is finite for a specified diagram. It enumerates the compressed lattice support and checks the area identity; it does not certify the analytic transfer, eventual threshold, or nefness conclusion.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of [d,H,t,k]; t is a rational string.
# PRECONDITIONS: positive integral d,H,k; t>1; (1+t)^2>d^2*H*t; bounded contract below.
# OUTPUTS: exact integer slices, compressed exponents, and rational triangle area; invalid case returns error.
# TIME: O(k*d*H + number of compressed exponents) rational operations per case.
# SPACE: O(number of slices + number of compressed exponents).
# ARITHMETIC: exact python-flint fmpq and Python integers; no floating point.
# FAILURE: malformed or out-of-contract input returns a precondition error for that case.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpq

def floor(v):
    return int(v.numerator) // int(v.denominator)

def ceil(v):
    return -floor(-v)

def compress(case):
    try:
        d, H, ts, k = case
        if any(type(v) is not int for v in (d,H,k)):
            raise ValueError()
        if not (1 <= d <= 4 and 1 <= H <= 4 and 1 <= k <= 8):
            raise ValueError()
        if not isinstance(ts,str) or len(ts)>32:
            raise ValueError()
        t = fmpq(ts)
        if not (1 < t <= 64 and (1+t)**2 > d*d*H*t):
            raise ValueError()
        W = d*H
        a, b, c = W*t/(1+t), W/(1+t), fmpq(1,d)
        rows, compressed = [], []
        for q in range(ceil(-k*b), floor(k*a)+1):
            lo = max(0, -q)
            length = k*c*(1+fmpq(q)/(k*b)) if q<0 else k*c*(1-fmpq(q)/(k*a))
            hi = floor(lo+length)
            if hi >= lo:
                rows.append([q,lo,hi])
                for h in range(hi-lo+1):
                    compressed.append([q,h])
        area = k*k*c*(a+b)/2
        return {'rows':rows, 'compressed':compressed, 'area':[int(area.numerator),int(area.denominator)]}
    except (ValueError,TypeError,ZeroDivisionError,OverflowError):
        return {'error':'precondition'}

payload = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
if not isinstance(payload,list) or len(payload)>128:
    print(json.dumps({'error':'input_contract'}))
else:
    print(json.dumps([compress(case) for case in payload],sort_keys=True,separators=(',',':')))
```

  "panel3": {

    "independent_reference": "Cartesian polygon membership and shoelace area provide an independent exact-arithmetic description of the same finite geometry.",
    "input_contract": "JSON list of at most 128 cases [d,H,t,k]. Positive integer d,H<=4 and k<=8; rational-string t of length<=32, 1<t<=64, and (1+t)^2>d^2Ht. These bounded rational inputs instantiate the finite diagram geometry; they do not establish a nodal divisor on a surface.",

    "oracle_code": "import json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction as Q\n\ndef cross(A,B,C):\n    return (B[0]-A[0])*(C[1]-A[1])-(B[1]-A[1])*(C[0]-A[0])\n\ndef inside(vertices, point):\n    return all(cross(vertices[i-1],vertices[i],point)>=0 for i in range(len(vertices)))\n\ndef geometry(record):\n    try:\n        degree, square, parameter, scale = record\n        assert all(type(x)==int for x in [degree,square,scale])\n        assert degree in range(1,5) and square in range(1,5) and scale in range(1,9)\n        assert type(parameter)==str and len(parameter)<=32\n        weight=Q(parameter)\n        assert weight>1 and weight<=64\n        assert weight*degree**2*square<(weight+1)**2\n    except (AssertionError,ValueError,TypeError,ZeroDivisionError):\n        return {'error':'precondition'}\n    left=Q(degree*square,1+weight)\n    right=left*weight\n    summit=Q(1,degree)\n    polygon=[(Q(0),Q(0)),(scale*right,Q(0)),(scale*summit,scale*summit),(Q(0),scale*left)]\n    triangle=[(-scale*left,Q(0)),(scale*right,Q(0)),(Q(0),scale*summit)]\n    buckets={}\n    # Deliberately enumerate Cartesian lattice points instead of using slice-length formulas.\n    cap=scale*degree*square\n    for alpha in range(cap+1):\n        for beta in range(cap+1):\n            if inside(polygon,(alpha,beta)):\n                buckets.setdefault(alpha-beta,[]).append(beta)\n    slices=[]\n    shifted=[]\n    for coordinate,heights in sorted(buckets.items()):\n        heights.sort()\n        assert heights==list(range(heights[0],heights[-1]+1))\n        slices.append([coordinate,heights[0],heights[-1]])\n        for height in heights:\n            point=[coordinate,height-heights[0]]\n            assert inside(triangle,point) # Re-check every returned lattice witness exactly.\n            shifted.append(point)\n    signed_area=sum(triangle[i-1][0]*triangle[i][1]-triangle[i][0]*triangle[i-1][1] for i in range(3))/2\n    assert signed_area==Q(scale**2*square,2)\n    return {'rows':slices,'compressed':shifted,'area':[signed_area.numerator,signed_area.denominator]}\n\nif __name__=='__main__':\n    try:\n        records=json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))\n        truth=[geometry(record) for record in records]\n        declared=json.loads(sys.argv[3])\n        result=json.load(open(sys.argv[2]))\n        assert declared==truth and result==truth\n    except Exception:\n        sys.exit(6)\n",
    "output_contract": "For each admissible case return the exact consecutive integer slices, their compressed exponents, and the exact rational area of the dilated triangle. Invalid cases return a precondition error.",
    "reason": "The diagonal-slice construction is finite for a specified diagram. It enumerates the compressed lattice support and checks the area identity; it does not certify the analytic transfer, eventual threshold, or nefness conclusion.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of [d,H,t,k]; t is a rational string.\n# PRECONDITIONS: positive integral d,H,k; t>1; (1+t)^2>d^2*H*t; bounded contract below.\n# OUTPUTS: exact integer slices, compressed exponents, and rational triangle area; invalid case returns error.\n# TIME: O(k*d*H + number of compressed exponents) rational operations per case.\n# SPACE: O(number of slices + number of compressed exponents).\n# ARITHMETIC: exact python-flint fmpq and Python integers; no floating point.\n# FAILURE: malformed or out-of-contract input returns a precondition error for that case.\nimport json, sys\nfrom flint import fmpq\n\ndef floor(v):\n    return int(v.numerator) // int(v.denominator)\n\ndef ceil(v):\n    return -floor(-v)\n\ndef compress(case):\n    try:\n        d, H, ts, k = case\n        if any(type(v) is not int for v in (d,H,k)):\n            raise ValueError()\n        if not (1 <= d <= 4 and 1 <= H <= 4 and 1 <= k <= 8):\n            raise ValueError()\n        if not isinstance(ts,str) or len(ts)>32:\n            raise ValueError()\n        t = fmpq(ts)\n        if not (1 < t <= 64 and (1+t)**2 > d*d*H*t):\n            raise ValueError()\n        W = d*H\n        a, b, c = W*t/(1+t), W/(1+t), fmpq(1,d)\n        rows, compressed = [], []\n        for q in range(ceil(-k*b), floor(k*a)+1):\n            lo = max(0, -q)\n            length = k*c*(1+fmpq(q)/(k*b)) if q<0 else k*c*(1-fmpq(q)/(k*a))\n            hi = floor(lo+length)\n            if hi >= lo:\n                rows.append([q,lo,hi])\n                for h in range(hi-lo+1):\n                    compressed.append([q,h])\n        area = k*k*c*(a+b)/2\n        return {'rows':rows, 'compressed':compressed, 'area':[int(area.numerator),int(area.denominator)]}\n    except (ValueError,TypeError,ZeroDivisionError,OverflowError):\n        return {'error':'precondition'}\n\npayload = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())\nif not isinstance(payload,list) or len(payload)>128:\n    print(json.dumps({'error':'input_contract'}))\nelse:\n    print(json.dumps([compress(case) for case in payload],sort_keys=True,separators=(',',':')))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Finite jet evaluation can support a mathematical software component that certifies injectivity for a supplied finite function space; the surface theorem does not supply a general Seshadri-constant solver. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a game or simulation model. The results concern algebraic surfaces and holomorphic jet tests. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A mathematical checking tool can expose jet-matrix construction and certified nonzero minors for finite inputs. Persistence under a limit requires the stated convergence hypotheses. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite jet test gives a procedure for a represented function space, and the triangle multiplicity estimate supplies a conditional mathematical bound. These finite mechanisms do not decide all curve inequalities on a surface. |
| data_structures | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The derivative table and finite exponent supports suggest representations for a jet checker. The paper gives mathematical indexing rather than a storage-performance result. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: an accelerator architecture or a hardware execution contract. Finite matrices alone do not establish an acceleration result. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a cryptographic primitive, threat model or security reduction. Finite torus subgroups serve interpolation tests. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a learning objective, statistical model or training guarantee. The jet tests are exact mathematical injectivity conditions. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a finite optimisation formulation or operations-research algorithm. The nef boundary condition quantifies over integral curves on a surface. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a communication protocol or distributed consistency model. Character averaging is a mathematical decomposition of Laurent polynomials. |
| other (algebraic_geometry) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The stated application is algebraic geometry: eventual maximality of multipoint Seshadri constants on polarized surfaces at very general tuples. Its use requires the full geometric hypotheses and the existence threshold. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The eventual maximality theorem requires a smooth integral complex projective surface and an ample line bundle. Blocked use: ['Applying eventual maximality when the geometric setting or ampleness is unestablished.']
- **L2** (nonconstructive): The threshold is allowed to depend on the polarized surface; eventual maximality does not supply a universal threshold for all polarized surfaces. Blocked use: ['Using a polarization-independent point-count threshold.']
- **L3** (hypothesis): Equality is asserted outside a countable union of proper closed subsets of the configuration space, rather than for every distinct tuple. Blocked use: ['Certifying equality for an arbitrary supplied tuple from distinctness alone.']
- **L4** (counterexample_scope): The sharp plane threshold and strict nonhomogeneous inequality are a separate refinement, not a consequence supplied by eventual maximality alone. Blocked use: ['Replacing the eventual surface theorem with the sharp plane refinement.']
- **L5** (formal_scope): The conclusion concerns a nef boundary class of self-intersection zero. Blocked use: ['Interpreting the boundary class as ample or as having positive self-intersection.']
- **L6** (formal_scope): Symplectic packing stability alone does not establish the required algebraic statement on the fixed complex surface. Blocked use: ['Using symplectic packing stability alone to certify nefness on the fixed complex blow-up.']
- **L7** (edge_case): The triangle subgroup jet test requires the strict critical-slope inequality. The equality case is not covered by this sufficient condition. Blocked use: ['Claiming jet injectivity at or below the critical slope from the subgroup test.']
- **L8** (nonconstructive): The analytic transfer permits auxiliary weights and dilations depending on the finite-dimensional space and makes no uniformity assertion across such spaces. Blocked use: ['Treating one analytic dilation as a uniform certificate for all section spaces.']
- **L9** (formal_scope): The quadrilateral bound controls initial exponents only. Blocked use: ['Bounding every Taylor coefficient or the full Taylor support by the quadrilateral.']
- **L10** (nonconstructive): The surface configurations transferred from torus tests may depend on the jet parameters. Simultaneous tests are obtained through the very general locus. Blocked use: ['Claiming that all transferred tests use one explicitly constructed surface tuple.']
- **L11** (hypothesis): The positive-square nonvanishing lemma also requires positive intersection with a nef real divisor class and sufficiently large multiples divisible by the divisor denominators. Blocked use: ['Concluding effectiveness from positive self-intersection alone or at an arbitrary small multiple.']
- **L12** (hypothesis): The ground-field extension assumes an uncountable algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Blocked use: ['Applying the ground-field corollary to countable fields, nonclosed fields or positive characteristic without another argument.']
- **L13** (formal_scope): Nefness from homogeneous jet tests assumes the absence of nonzero sections for all positive jet parameters above the critical slope. A finite collection of tests alone does not satisfy that hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Certifying the nef boundary from only a bounded finite suite of jet tests.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 039 (`lean/docs/039.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Nagata's conjecture for plane curves](../../preprints/Nagatas-Conjecture-for-Plane-Curves-September-23-2026/main.pdf)
> - [Maximal Seshadri constants on arbitrary polarized surfaces](../../preprints/Maximal-Seshadri-Constants-on-Arbitrary-Polarized-Surfaces-September-23-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Nagata's conjecture asserts that a nonzero effective plane curve of degree $d$, with multiplicities at least $m_i$ at $r\ge10$ very general points, satisfies $\sum_i m_i<d\sqrt r$.
>
> The formalization establishes this inequality simultaneously for all curves and multiplicity vectors outside one countable union of proper Zariski-closed exceptional sets with nonempty complement. Reducible curves, repeated components, and unequal multiplicities are included, in both effective-curve and homogeneous-polynomial formulations. The formalization also includes the passage from homogeneous effective cycles to equations.
>
> The maximality question asks whether the multipoint Seshadri constant reaches the upper bound $\sqrt{L^2/r}$ at very general points once $r$ is sufficiently large. The formalized result establishes this for every smooth integral complex projective surface $S$ and ample line bundle $L$.
>
> For every $r$ above a threshold depending on $(S,L)$, it gives a countable union of proper closed exceptional sets with nonempty complement. Outside it, the constant is $\sqrt{L^2/r}$ and the boundary class defining this Seshadri constant on the point blowup is nef. The threshold is not explicit or uniform over all surfaces.

import Mathlib

namespace OAI



                                                                            
                                                                               
                                                                   
namespace MaximalSeshadri.Geometry
noncomputable section
open CategoryTheory AlgebraicGeometry TopologicalSpace
open scoped AlgebraicGeometry

attribute [local instance] MvPolynomial.gradedAlgebra

                                                                                    
def complexProjectiveSpace (N : ℕ) : Scheme :=
  Proj (MvPolynomial.homogeneousSubmodule (Fin (N + 1)) ℂ)

                                                                              
def constantsInDegreeZero (N : ℕ) :
    ℂ →+* (MvPolynomial.homogeneousSubmodule (Fin (N + 1)) ℂ 0) :=
  { toFun := fun c => ⟨MvPolynomial.C c, MvPolynomial.isHomogeneous_C _ c⟩
    map_one' := Subtype.ext MvPolynomial.C_1
    map_mul' := fun a b => Subtype.ext (MvPolynomial.C_mul (a := a) (a' := b))
    map_zero' := Subtype.ext MvPolynomial.C_0
    map_add' := fun a b => Subtype.ext (MvPolynomial.C_add (a := a) (a' := b)) }

                                                           
def projectiveSpaceToSpec (N : ℕ) :
    complexProjectiveSpace N ⟶ Spec (CommRingCat.of ℂ) :=
  Proj.toSpecZero _ ≫ Spec.map (CommRingCat.ofHom (constantsInDegreeZero N))

                                                                             
                                                                            
                                          
structure Surface where
  scheme : Scheme
  structureMap : scheme ⟶ Spec (CommRingCat.of ℂ)
  integral : IsIntegral scheme
  smooth : SmoothOfRelativeDimension 2 structureMap
  embeddingDimension : ℕ
  embedding : scheme ⟶ complexProjectiveSpace embeddingDimension
  closedImmersion : IsClosedImmersion embedding
  overComplex : embedding ≫ projectiveSpaceToSpec embeddingDimension = structureMap

attribute [instance] Surface.integral Surface.smooth Surface.closedImmersion

variable (X : Scheme)

                                                          
structure LineBundle where
  sheaf : X.Modules
  locallyRankOne : ∀ x : X, ∃ U : X.Opens, x ∈ U ∧
    Nonempty (sheaf.restrict U.ι ≅ SheafOfModules.unit U.toScheme.ringCatSheaf)

                                                                           
                                                                              
def moduleTensor (M N : X.Modules) : X.Modules :=
  (PresheafOfModules.sheafification (𝟙 X.ringCatSheaf.obj)).obj
    (PresheafOfModulesOfCommRing.Monoidal.tensorObj (R := X.presheaf) M.val N.val)

                                                        
def modulePow (M : X.Modules) : ℕ → X.Modules
  | 0 => SheafOfModules.unit X.ringCatSheaf
  | n + 1 => moduleTensor X M (modulePow M n)

                                                                      
def structureSheaf : X.Modules := SheafOfModules.unit X.ringCatSheaf

                                                                        
abbrev GlobalSections (M : X.Modules) := structureSheaf X ⟶ M

                                                                                
                                                                         
def sectionOpen {M : X.Modules} (s : GlobalSections X M) : X.Opens :=
  ⨆ (U : X.Opens) (_ : IsIso ((Scheme.Modules.restrictFunctor U.ι).map s)), U

                                                                         
def LineBundle.IsAmple (L : LineBundle X) : Prop :=
  ∀ (x : X) (V : X.Opens), x ∈ V → ∃ n : ℕ, 0 < n ∧
    ∃ s : GlobalSections X (modulePow X L.sheaf n),
      x ∈ sectionOpen X s ∧ sectionOpen X s ≤ V ∧ IsAffineOpen (sectionOpen X s)

end
end MaximalSeshadri.Geometry

namespace MaximalSeshadri.Geometry
noncomputable section
open CategoryTheory AlgebraicGeometry TopologicalSpace
open scoped AlgebraicGeometry

variable (X : Scheme)

                                                       
def restrictScalar (U : X.Opens) : Γ(X, ⊤) →+* Γ(X, U) :=
  (X.presheaf.map (homOfLE le_top).op).hom

lemma restrictScalar_naturality {U V : X.Opens} (i : U ⟶ V) (r : Γ(X, ⊤)) :
    X.presheaf.map i.op (restrictScalar X V r) = restrictScalar X U r := by
  change (X.presheaf.map (homOfLE le_top).op ≫ X.presheaf.map i.op) r = _
  rw [← X.presheaf.map_comp]
  rfl

                                                        
def sheafHomSMul {M N : X.Modules} (r : Γ(X, ⊤)) (f : M ⟶ N) : M ⟶ N where
  val := {
    app U := by
      letI : Module Γ(X, U.unop) (M.val.obj U) := (M.val.obj U).isModule
      letI : Module Γ(X, U.unop) (N.val.obj U) := (N.val.obj U).isModule
      let g : M.val.obj U →ₗ[Γ(X, U.unop)] N.val.obj U := (f.val.app U).hom
      exact ModuleCat.ofHom ((restrictScalar X U.unop r) • g)
    naturality {U V} g := by
      let : Module (X.presheaf.obj V) (N.presheaf.obj V) := (N.val.obj V).isModule
      ext m
      change restrictScalar X V.unop r • f.app V.unop (M.presheaf.map g m) =
        N.presheaf.map g (restrictScalar X U.unop r • f.app U.unop m)
      have hn := CategoryTheory.congr_fun (f.mapPresheaf.naturality g) m
      change f.app V.unop (M.presheaf.map g m) =
        N.presheaf.map g (f.app U.unop m) at hn
      rw [hn]
      calc
        _ = X.presheaf.map g (restrictScalar X U.unop r) •
            N.presheaf.map g (f.app U.unop m) := by
              exact congrArg (fun a : X.presheaf.obj V =>
                a • N.presheaf.map g (f.app U.unop m))
                (restrictScalar_naturality X g.unop r).symm
        _ = _ := (N.map_smul g.unop (restrictScalar X U.unop r) (f.app U.unop m)).symm }


instance sheafHomModule (M N : X.Modules) : Module Γ(X, ⊤) (M ⟶ N) where
  smul := sheafHomSMul X
  one_smul f := by
    ext U m
    change restrictScalar X U 1 • f.app U m = f.app U m
    simp
  mul_smul r s f := by
    ext U m
    change restrictScalar X U (r * s) • f.app U m =
      restrictScalar X U r • (restrictScalar X U s • f.app U m)
    simp [mul_smul]
  smul_zero r := by
    ext U m
    change restrictScalar X U r • (0 : Γ(N, U)) = 0
    exact smul_zero _
  smul_add r f g := by
    ext U m
    change restrictScalar X U r • (f.app U m + g.app U m) =
      restrictScalar X U r • f.app U m + restrictScalar X U r • g.app U m
    exact smul_add _ _ _
  zero_smul f := by
    ext U m
    change restrictScalar X U 0 • f.app U m = 0
    simp
  add_smul r s f := by
    ext U m
    change restrictScalar X U (r + s) • f.app U m =
      restrictScalar X U r • f.app U m + restrictScalar X U s • f.app U m
    simp [add_smul]

instance sheafLinear : Linear Γ(X, ⊤) X.Modules where
  smul_comp M N P r f g := by
    ext U m
    change g.app U (restrictScalar X U r • f.app U m) =
      restrictScalar X U r • g.app U (f.app U m)
    exact g.app_smul _ _
  comp_smul M N P f r g := by
    ext U m
    rfl

end
end MaximalSeshadri.Geometry

namespace MaximalSeshadri.Geometry
noncomputable section
open CategoryTheory AlgebraicGeometry TopologicalSpace
open scoped AlgebraicGeometry

variable {X : Scheme.{0}}

instance schemeHasExt : HasExt.{1} X.Modules := HasExt.standard X.Modules

                                                                           
                       
def baseScalars (f : X ⟶ Spec (CommRingCat.of ℂ)) : ℂ →+* Γ(X, ⊤) :=
  f.appTop.hom.comp (Scheme.ΓSpecIso (CommRingCat.of ℂ)).inv.hom

                                                                      
                                                           

                                                                            
                                                                          
                                                                      
abbrev cohomology (M : X.Modules) (n : ℕ) : Type 1 :=
  Abelian.Ext.{1} (C := X.Modules) (structureSheaf X) M n

                                                                           
                                                                               
                                           
def cohomologyDimension (f : X ⟶ Spec (CommRingCat.of ℂ))
    (M : X.Modules) (n : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  letI : Module ℂ (cohomology M n) :=
    Module.compHom (cohomology M n) (baseScalars f)
  Module.finrank ℂ (cohomology M n)

                                                                              
                                                                           
def eulerCharacteristic (f : X ⟶ Spec (CommRingCat.of ℂ)) (d : ℕ)
    (M : X.Modules) : ℤ :=
  ∑ n ∈ Finset.range (d + 1), (-1 : ℤ) ^ n * (cohomologyDimension f M n : ℤ)

                                                                           
                                             
def selfIntersection (S : Surface) (L : LineBundle S.scheme) : ℤ :=
  eulerCharacteristic S.structureMap 2 (modulePow S.scheme L.sheaf 2) -
    2 * eulerCharacteristic S.structureMap 2 L.sheaf +
    eulerCharacteristic S.structureMap 2 (SheafOfModules.unit S.scheme.ringCatSheaf)

                                                                          
                                                                      
structure IntegralCurve (S : Surface) where
  scheme : Scheme
  embedding : scheme ⟶ S.scheme
  closedImmersion : IsClosedImmersion embedding
  integral : IsIntegral scheme
  dimension : topologicalKrullDim scheme = 1

attribute [instance] IntegralCurve.closedImmersion IntegralCurve.integral

                                                                    
                             
def curveDegree (S : Surface) (L : LineBundle S.scheme) (C : IntegralCurve S) : ℤ :=
  eulerCharacteristic (C.embedding ≫ S.structureMap) 1
    ((Scheme.Modules.pullback C.embedding).obj L.sheaf) -
  eulerCharacteristic (C.embedding ≫ S.structureMap) 1
    (SheafOfModules.unit C.scheme.ringCatSheaf)

end
end MaximalSeshadri.Geometry

namespace MaximalSeshadri.Geometry
noncomputable section
open CategoryTheory CategoryTheory.Limits AlgebraicGeometry TopologicalSpace
open scoped AlgebraicGeometry

                               
abbrev complexBase : Scheme := Spec (CommRingCat.of ℂ)

                                                                           
abbrev ComplexPoint (S : Surface) :=
  Over.mk (𝟙 complexBase) ⟶ Over.mk S.structureMap

                                                             
def ComplexPoint.image {S : Surface} (p : ComplexPoint S) : S.scheme :=
  p.left (⟨⊥, Ideal.isPrime_bot⟩ : PrimeSpectrum ℂ)

                                                                                   
def surfacePower (S : Surface) (r : ℕ) : Over complexBase :=
  ∏ᶜ (fun _ : Fin r => Over.mk S.structureMap)

                                                                 
def tupleImage (S : Surface) (r : ℕ) (p : Fin r → ComplexPoint S) :
    (surfacePower S r).left :=
  (Limits.Pi.lift p).left (⟨⊥, Ideal.isPrime_bot⟩ : PrimeSpectrum ℂ)

                                                                               
                                        
@[instance_reducible] def tupleZariskiTopology (S : Surface) (r : ℕ) :
    TopologicalSpace (Fin r → ComplexPoint S) :=
  TopologicalSpace.induced (tupleImage S r) inferInstance

                                                                
def Configuration (S : Surface) (r : ℕ) :=
  {p : Fin r → ComplexPoint S // Function.Injective (fun i => (p i).image)}

instance configurationTopology (S : Surface) (r : ℕ) :
    TopologicalSpace (Configuration S r) :=
  letI := tupleZariskiTopology S r
  inferInstanceAs (TopologicalSpace {p : Fin r → ComplexPoint S //
    Function.Injective (fun i => (p i).image)})

                                                                     
                                                                     
                                                                        
                                                                       
def idealOrder {R : Type} [CommRing R] [IsLocalRing R] (I : Ideal R) : ℕ :=
  sInf {n : ℕ | ¬ I ≤ IsLocalRing.maximalIdeal R ^ (n + 1)}

                                                                             
                                                                             
                                                      
def curveMultiplicity (S : Surface) (C : IntegralCurve S) (p : ComplexPoint S) : ℕ := by
  classical
  exact if h : ∃ q : C.scheme, C.embedding q = p.image then
    idealOrder (RingHom.ker (C.embedding.stalkMap h.choose).hom)
  else 0

                                                              
def totalMultiplicity (S : Surface) (r : ℕ) (C : IntegralCurve S)
    (p : Configuration S r) : ℕ :=
  ∑ i : Fin r, curveMultiplicity S C (p.val i)

                                                                 
def seshadriConstant (S : Surface) (L : LineBundle S.scheme) (r : ℕ)
    (p : Configuration S r) : ℝ :=
  sInf {a : ℝ | ∃ C : IntegralCurve S, 0 < totalMultiplicity S r C p ∧
    a = (curveDegree S L C : ℝ) / (totalMultiplicity S r C p : ℝ)}

                                                                               
                                                                               
                                                                               
                             

end
end MaximalSeshadri.Geometry

namespace MaximalSeshadri.Geometry
noncomputable section
open CategoryTheory CategoryTheory.Limits AlgebraicGeometry TopologicalSpace
open scoped AlgebraicGeometry

                                                                      
                                                                            
                              
def moduleCurveDegree (S : Surface) (M : S.scheme.Modules) (C : IntegralCurve S) : ℤ :=
  eulerCharacteristic (C.embedding ≫ S.structureMap) 1
    ((Scheme.Modules.pullback C.embedding).obj M) -
  eulerCharacteristic (C.embedding ≫ S.structureMap) 1
    (structureSheaf C.scheme)

                                                                             
                            
def sectionImageIdeal {X : Scheme} (J : LineBundle X)
    (ι : J.sheaf ⟶ structureSheaf X) (U : X.Opens) : Ideal Γ(X, U) := by
  letI : Module Γ(X, U) (J.sheaf.val.obj (Opposite.op U)) :=
    (J.sheaf.val.obj (Opposite.op U)).isModule
  let φ : J.sheaf.val.obj (Opposite.op U) →ₗ[Γ(X, U)] Γ(X, U) :=
    (ι.val.app (Opposite.op U)).hom
  exact φ.range

                                                                            
                                                                            
                                                                                 
def PresentsPullbackIdeal {X Y : Scheme} (I : X.IdealSheafData) (f : Y ⟶ X)
    (J : LineBundle Y) (ι : J.sheaf ⟶ structureSheaf Y) : Prop :=
  Mono ι ∧ ∀ (U : Y.affineOpens) (V : X.affineOpens)
    (e : U.1 ≤ f ⁻¹ᵁ V.1),
    sectionImageIdeal J ι U.1 = (I.ideal V).map (f.appLE V.1 U.1 e).hom

                                                            
def InvertiblePullbackIdeal {X Y : Scheme} (I : X.IdealSheafData) (f : Y ⟶ X) : Prop :=
  ∃ (J : LineBundle Y) (ι : J.sheaf ⟶ structureSheaf Y), PresentsPullbackIdeal I f J ι

                                                                             
                                                                            
                                                           
def centreIdeal (S : Surface) (r : ℕ) (p : Configuration S r) : S.scheme.IdealSheafData :=
  ∏ i : Fin r, (p.val i).left.ker

                                                                            
                                                                         
                                                       
def IsBlowup {X B : Scheme} (I : X.IdealSheafData) (π : B ⟶ X) : Prop :=
  InvertiblePullbackIdeal I π ∧ ∀ (Y : Scheme) (f : Y ⟶ X),
    InvertiblePullbackIdeal I f → ∃! h : Y ⟶ B, h ≫ π = f

                                                                         
                                                                            
                                                        
structure PointBlowup (S : Surface) (r : ℕ) (p : Configuration S r) where
  surface : Surface
  projection : surface.scheme ⟶ S.scheme
  overComplex : projection ≫ S.structureMap = surface.structureMap
  isBlowup : IsBlowup (centreIdeal S r p) projection
  exceptionalIdeal : Fin r → LineBundle surface.scheme
  exceptionalInclusion : ∀ i, (exceptionalIdeal i).sheaf ⟶ structureSheaf surface.scheme
  exceptionalPullback : ∀ i, PresentsPullbackIdeal (p.val i).left.ker projection
    (exceptionalIdeal i) (exceptionalInclusion i)

                                                           
                                                                              
                                                                        
def PointBlowup.BoundaryNef {S : Surface} {r : ℕ} {p : Configuration S r}
    (B : PointBlowup S r p) (L : LineBundle S.scheme) : Prop :=
  ∀ C : IntegralCurve B.surface,
    0 ≤ (moduleCurveDegree B.surface
      ((Scheme.Modules.pullback B.projection).obj L.sheaf) C : ℝ) +
      Real.sqrt ((selfIntersection S L : ℝ) / r) *
        ∑ i : Fin r, (curveDegree B.surface (B.exceptionalIdeal i) C : ℝ)

                                                                           
                                                                        
def EventualMaximalSeshadri (S : Surface) (L : LineBundle S.scheme) : Prop :=
  ∃ r₀ : ℕ, 0 < r₀ ∧ ∀ r : ℕ, r₀ ≤ r →
    ∃ Z : ℕ → Set (Configuration S r),
      (∀ n, IsClosed (Z n) ∧ Z n ≠ Set.univ) ∧
      (∃ p : Configuration S r, ∀ n, p ∉ Z n) ∧
      ∀ p : Configuration S r, (∀ n, p ∉ Z n) →
        (∃ B : PointBlowup S r p, B.BoundaryNef L) ∧
        seshadriConstant S L r p = Real.sqrt ((selfIntersection S L : ℝ) / r)

end
end MaximalSeshadri.Geometry

namespace MaximalSeshadri.Geometry

theorem maximalSeshadriConstants (S : Surface) (L : LineBundle S.scheme)
    (hL : LineBundle.IsAmple S.scheme L) : EventualMaximalSeshadri S L := by
  sorry

end MaximalSeshadri.Geometry

end OAI

      "statement_lean": "def sheafHomSMul {M N : X.Modules} (r : Γ(X, ⊤)) (f : M ⟶ N) : M ⟶ N where\n  val := {\n    app U := by\n      letI : Module Γ(X, U.unop) (M.val.obj U) := (M.val.obj U).isModule\n      letI : Module Γ(X, U.unop) (N.val.obj U) := (N.val.obj U).isModule\n      let g : M.val.obj U →ₗ[Γ(X, U.unop)] N.val.obj U := (f.val.app U).hom\n      exact ModuleCat.ofHom ((restrictScalar X U.unop r) • g)\n    naturality {U V} g := by\n      let : Module (X.presheaf.obj V) (N.presheaf.obj V) := (N.val.obj V).isModule\n      ext m\n      change restrictScalar X V.unop r • f.app V.unop (M.presheaf.map g m) =\n        N.presheaf.map g (restrictScalar X U.unop r • f.app U.unop m)\n      have hn := CategoryTheory.congr_fun (f.mapPresheaf.naturality g) m\n      change f.app V.unop (M.presheaf.map g m) =\n        N.presheaf.map g (f.app U.unop m) at hn\n      rw [hn]\n      calc\n        _ = X.presheaf.map g (restrictScalar X U.unop r) •\n            N.presheaf.map g (f.app U.unop m) := by\n              exact congrArg (fun a : X.presheaf.obj V =>\n                a • N.presheaf.map g (f.app U.unop m))\n                (restrictScalar_naturality X g.unop r).symm\n        _ = _ := (N.map_smul g.unop (restrictScalar X U.unop r) (f.app U.unop m)).symm }"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "instance",
      "name": "sheafHomModule",
      "statement_lean": "instance sheafHomModule (M N : X.Modules) : Module Γ(X, ⊤) (M ⟶ N) where\n  smul := sheafHomSMul X\n  one_smul f := by\n    ext U m\n    change restrictScalar X U 1 • f.app U m = f.app U m\n    simp\n  mul_smul r s f := by\n    ext U m\n    change restrictScalar X U (r * s) • f.app U m =\n      restrictScalar X U r • (restrictScalar X U s • f.app U m)\n    simp [mul_smul]\n  smul_zero r := by\n    ext U m\n    change restrictScalar X U r • (0 : Γ(N, U)) = 0\n    exact smul_zero _\n  smul_add r f g := by\n    ext U m\n    change restrictScalar X U r • (f.app U m + g.app U m) =\n      restrictScalar X U r • f.app U m + restrictScalar X U r • g.app U m\n    exact smul_add _ _ _\n  zero_smul f := by\n    ext U m\n    change restrictScalar X U 0 • f.app U m = 0\n    simp\n  add_smul r s f := by\n    ext U m\n    change restrictScalar X U (r + s) • f.app U m =\n      restrictScalar X U r • f.app U m + restrictScalar X U s • f.app U m\n    simp [add_smul]"
    },

    "doc": "lean/docs/039.md",
    "family": "039",
    "text": "The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):\n\n- [Nagata's conjecture for plane curves](../../preprints/Nagatas-Conjecture-for-Plane-Curves-September-23-2026/main.pdf)\n- [Maximal Seshadri constants on arbitrary polarized surfaces](../../preprints/Maximal-Seshadri-Constants-on-Arbitrary-Polarized-Surfaces-September-23-2026/main.pdf)\n\n## Scope\n\nNagata's conjecture asserts that a nonzero effective plane curve of degree $d$, with multiplicities at least $m_i$ at $r\\ge10$ very general points, satisfies $\\sum_i m_i<d\\sqrt r$.\n\nThe formalization establishes this inequality simultaneously for all curves and multiplicity vectors outside one countable union of proper Zariski-closed exceptional sets with nonempty complement. Reducible curves, repeated components, and unequal multiplicities are included, in both effective-curve and homogeneous-polynomial formulations. The formalization also includes the passage from homogeneous effective cycles to equations.\n\nThe maximality question asks whether the multipoint Seshadri constant reaches the upper bound $\\sqrt{L^2/r}$ at very general points once $r$ is sufficiently large. The formalized result establishes this for every smooth integral complex projective surface $S$ and ample line bundle $L$.\n\nFor every $r$ above a threshold depending on $(S,L)$, it gives a countable union of proper closed exceptional sets with nonempty complement. Outside it, the constant is $\\sqrt{L^2/r}$ and the boundary class defining this Seshadri constant on the point blowup is nef. The threshold is not explicit or uniform over all surfaces."
  },

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that, for every smooth integral complex projective surface <i>S</i> and every ample line bundle <i>L</i>, the multipoint Seshadri constant at <i>r</i> very general points equals $`\sqrt{L^2/r}`$ for every sufficiently large integer <i>r</i>. This resolves positively the qualitative Nagata–Biran conjecture for surfaces.
